MASTER PROMPT — GHOST DATA STILL EXISTS: BUILD THE COMPLETE HACKERRANK SOLUTION
You are my senior AI engineer, Python developer, data-forensics specialist, and competition solution architect.
Your task is to build a complete, accurate, runnable, and evaluated solution for the HackerRank challenge “Ghost Data Still Exists” from the repository:
https://github.com/interviewstreet/hackerrank-orchestrate-october26
I want you to work directly inside the repository and complete the implementation, testing, evaluation, and submission preparation.
Do not stop at explaining the solution or giving sample code. Inspect the actual files, implement the system, run it, debug it, and generate the final deliverables.
Work autonomously where possible. Ask me questions only when an essential decision cannot be resolved from the repository, problem statement, dataset, or environment.

1. UNDERSTAND THE PROBLEM
A company believes it has deleted a customer's personal data, but the data may still exist in secondary databases, support attachments, search indexes, analytics exports, logs, archives, backups, and other systems.
Synchronization jobs, imports, and restore operations can recreate information that was previously deleted.
Build an evidence-driven AI-powered deletion audit agent that investigates every supplied deletion request and determines the actual deletion state.
For each case, the agent must:
2. Identify the person using current and historical identifiers.
3. Locate all relevant records and personal information.
4. Inspect evidence from CSV files and local files, including PDFs, images, logs, JSON, and SQLite-compatible database snapshots.
5. Determine the state of records at the requested `audit_at` timestamp.
6. Apply the rules in `deletion_rules.csv`.
7. Identify active synchronization, import, export, indexing, or restore processes that could expose or recreate deleted data.
8. Determine whether deletion is complete, incomplete, or not applicable.
9. Generate ordered, evidence-backed findings, remediation actions, and verification checks.
10. Produce the required `output.csv` for every deletion request.

The original workflow's reported status is a claim, not proof. Never trust `reported_status` or an operation result without checking the available evidence.
Do not hardcode case IDs, expected labels, customer emails, resource IDs, hidden answers, or file-specific predictions. The system must generalize across the complete dataset and unseen evaluation cases.
2. READ THE OFFICIAL SPECIFICATION FIRST
Start by inspecting:

* `problem_statement.md`
* The repository README
* The chat transcript logging instructions
* The complete repository tree
* All relevant scripts, configuration, requirements, and dataset files

Use the official problem statement as the source of truth for the output schema, permitted operations, sorting rules, evidence requirements, and evaluation criteria.
Do not invent undocumented output fields or unsupported operation names.
If any implementation detail is unclear, inspect the actual source files and supporting evidence before making a decision.
3. INSPECT THE REAL DATASET
Find and inspect the actual files under `dataset/`, including:

* `deletion_requests.csv`
* `sample_deletion_requests.csv`
* `sample_output.csv`
* `user_identities.csv`
* `user_audit_log.csv`
* `system_activity.csv`
* `deletion_rules.csv`
* `evidence_files.csv`
* `output.csv`
* `dataset/evidence/` and all relevant nested files

First determine the exact directory structure and column schemas. Do not assume that file paths, columns, formats, or record counts match your expectations without checking them.
Understand how the evidence manifest links individual files to their systems, resources, and identifiers.
Inventory the evidence formats and determine which tools and libraries are needed to inspect them.
For evidence that cannot be interpreted as ordinary text, use appropriate parsers or OCR where useful. Inspect image evidence and database snapshots rather than ignoring them.
Never silently skip an evidence file that may affect a deletion decision. Record unsupported formats or parsing failures and investigate alternative ways to extract their contents.
The full dataset is available locally. Use the actual data, not invented examples, to build and validate the system.
4. IMPLEMENT ROBUST IDENTITY RESOLUTION
Create a general identity-resolution component.
It must:

* Match deletion requests to current identities.
* Follow historical changes recorded in `user_audit_log.csv`.
* Identify previous email addresses and other relevant identifiers.
* Link historical identifiers to the correct stable user ID.
* Handle email changes, duplicate identifiers, missing fields, and ambiguous matches carefully.
* Avoid accidentally merging two different users.
* Search for all identifiers that remain relevant to the deletion request across evidence files and system records.

Do not rely only on the email in `deletion_requests.csv`.
Use stable internal identifiers when supported by the evidence. Record the provenance of identity matches so that conclusions can be traced back to evidence.
If identity cannot be resolved confidently, do not fabricate a match. Use the available evidence and the specification to determine the defensible result.
5. IMPLEMENT EVIDENCE COLLECTION AND RESOURCE-STATE RECONSTRUCTION
Create an evidence-processing layer that collects relevant evidence for each case.
At minimum, examine:

* Current account records.
* Secondary databases.
* Support records and attachments.
* Search indexes.
* Analytics exports.
* Logs.
* Archives.
* Backups.
* Synchronization and import jobs.
* Restore operations and restore risks.
* Account tombstones.
* Policy-required retained records.
* Any other relevant resource represented in the actual dataset.

Resolve resource states as of `audit_at`, using event timestamps, available snapshots, system activity, and supporting evidence.
Do not assume that the latest row in a CSV represents the state at the audit timestamp.
Reconstruct state transitions where the evidence permits it. For example:

1. A customer record exists.
2. A deletion job runs.
3. The account service reports deletion.
4. A synchronization process runs afterward.
5. A secondary system recreates the record.
6. The deletion audit occurs.

The result must reflect the actual state supported by the timeline.
Distinguish between:

* A record that currently exists.
* A record that was deleted.
* A record that was recreated.
* A record that remains but has been redacted.
* A record that has been anonymized.
* A record that must be retained.
* A record whose state cannot be established reliably.

Treat activity logs and operation results as evidence to evaluate, not automatically as proof that the intended outcome occurred.
Every factual finding and completed-state verification must cite a valid evidence identifier permitted by the specification: a `file_id`, `activity_id`, or `audit_id`.
Use identifiers from the actual evidence manifest and source records. Never invent evidence IDs.
6. APPLY `deletion_rules.csv` EXACTLY
Read and interpret the actual deletion policy before implementing decision logic.
For each relevant data category, determine whether the policy requires:

* Complete deletion.
* Redaction of identifying fields.
* Anonymization.
* Retention.
* Some other explicitly documented treatment.

Do not replace the supplied policy with generic assumptions about privacy law.
Important: an account tombstone may need to remain so permitted records can continue referencing the stable internal `user_id`. However, direct identifiers such as name, email, phone number, and email hash must be removed when the supplied rules require their removal.
A retained record is not automatically a failure. Check whether its remaining fields are permitted by the rules and whether the required record remains available.
Conversely, deleting every record is not necessarily correct: the agent must preserve records that the policy requires the organization to retain.
Implement the policy as structured, data-driven rules wherever possible rather than scattering case-specific conditions throughout the code.
7. DETECT DATA-FLOW AND RESTORE RISKS
Identify active processes that can copy, recreate, or restore the user's data.
Investigate synchronization jobs, imports, exports, indexing, backup restoration, and other relevant processes present in the supplied evidence.
A deletion must not be considered complete merely because the primary account record is absent.
If an active data flow can recreate prohibited data, report the finding and propose the required remediation.
If a backup or restore path can restore deleted personal information, report the risk and propose the appropriate blocking action.
Only report a flow or restore risk as active when the evidence and policy support that conclusion. Do not invent risks merely because a system category exists.
The verification plan must establish that relevant flows are disabled and restore risks are blocked where required.
8. IMPLEMENT THE THREE REQUIRED STATUSES
Every request must receive exactly one status:
COMPLETE
Use `COMPLETE` only when all required conditions are established:

* Every relevant copy has received its policy-required treatment.
* No active prohibited data flow can recreate deleted data.
* Relevant restore risks have been controlled.
* Retained records contain only the identifiers permitted by policy.
* Records that must be retained remain available.

For `COMPLETE`, include only verification steps that prove the current state. Do not include findings or proposed remediation.
Each verification step must cite valid supporting evidence.
Do not classify a case as complete simply because the reported workflow status says `COMPLETE`.
INCOMPLETE
Use `INCOMPLETE` when evidence establishes that required deletion, redaction, anonymization, data-flow control, or restore protection is missing or incorrect.
Include, in order:

1. All relevant findings.
2. The complete ordered remediation plan.
3. The verification checks that must pass after remediation.

Findings must reference actual evidence.
Proposed remediation and future verification steps must use an empty string for `evidence_id`, as required by the official specification.
Do not claim that proposed remediation has already happened.
NOT_APPLICABLE
Use `NOT_APPLICABLE` when the person did not request deletion and the supplied request evidence supports that conclusion.
For these cases, `steps_json` must be exactly an empty JSON array: `[]`.
Do not initiate unauthorized deletion or verification.
9. FOLLOW THE EXACT OUTPUT SCHEMA
The final `output.csv` must contain exactly these columns:
`case_id,deletion_status,steps_json`
It must contain exactly one data row per row in `dataset/deletion_requests.csv`.

Do not silently omit requests, duplicate cases, or fabricate missing cases.

Every step object must contain exactly the required fields:

* `step_no`

* `evidence_id`

Use the exact allowed values specified in `problem_statement.md`.
Valid phases:

* `FINDING`

* `VERIFICATION`

Use the operation and state combinations defined in the official allowed-operations table.
Step numbers must start at 1 and increase consecutively.
Ordering requirements:

1. Findings first.
2. Remediation second.
3. Verification last.

Within each phase, follow the operation order in the official table, followed by `system_id` and `resource_id` in ascending order.
Preserve the required operation order and do not sort steps arbitrarily after generation.
For findings and verification in complete cases, use the appropriate actual `file_id`, `activity_id`, or `audit_id`.
For proposed remediation and future verification steps in incomplete cases, use `evidence_id: ""`.
Escape JSON correctly when writing it into CSV. Use a proper CSV writer instead of manually concatenating strings.
Do not include Markdown, explanations, extra columns, or debug messages in `output.csv`.
10. DESIGN THE SOLUTION FOR ACCURACY AND GENERALIZATION
Use a modular Python implementation unless the existing repository establishes a better approach.
Recommended logical components:

These are suggested responsibilities, not mandatory filenames. Adapt them to the existing repository.

Cache parsed evidence and reuse it across requests to avoid repeatedly scanning the same files.

# Ghost Data Still Exists

Northstar Shop (an imaginary company) lost **$2.4 million** after one supposedly deleted customer came back as a ghost in its systems. Every internal dashboard showed the deletion request as **“Complete.”** Weeks later, the same customer tried to create a new account. The platform refused: **“This email is already registered.”**

What looked like a simple signup bug exposed a much larger failure. The investigation uncovered personal data across secondary databases, support attachments, search indexes, analytics exports, logs, archives, and backups. A synchronization job had even recreated information that the primary account service had successfully deleted.

Northstar Shop now needs an AI-powered deletion audit agent that can prove whether a user's personal data is truly gone before another failure causes financial and reputational damage.

For deletion requests, the agent must investigate the supplied records, determine the user's current and historical identifiers, reconstruct what happened across systems, and decide whether the deletion is actually complete.

When data remains, the agent must produce the ordered remediation steps required to remove, redact, or anonymize it. When deletion has already been completed, the agent must produce the checks that prove it. The agent must also preserve records that the supplied policy requires the organization to retain.

Some systems preserve an account tombstone so permitted records can continue to reference the same internal `user_id`. In these records, the stable user reference may remain, but direct identifiers such as name, email, phone, and email hash must be removed.

## What You Need to Build

For each request, build a system that determines:

- whether the deletion is `COMPLETE`, `INCOMPLETE`, or `NOT_APPLICABLE` because the person did not request deletion;
- which records or identifiers still expose the user;
- which active processes can recreate deleted data;
- the ordered remediation steps required to complete the request.

A deletion is complete only when:

- every relevant copy has received the treatment required by `deletion_rules.csv`;
- no active sync, import, or restore path can recreate the data;
- retained records contain only the identifiers permitted by the rules; and
- records that must be retained remain available.

## Files Provided

All participant-facing files are inside `dataset/`.

| File | Contents |
| --- | --- |
| `deletion_requests.csv` | Requests that must be audited |
| `sample_deletion_requests.csv` | Sample requests |
| `sample_output.csv` | Correct answers for the sample requests |
| `user_identities.csv` | Current user identities |
| `user_audit_log.csv` | Historical identity changes |
| `system_activity.csv` | Deletion, sync, export, backup, restore, indexing, and related operational activity |
| `deletion_rules.csv` | Required treatment for each data category |
| `evidence_files.csv` | Manifest of the supplied evidence files |
| `output.csv` | Blank submission template |

Evidence may be stored as PDFs, images, logs, JSON, or SQLite-compatible SQL snapshot exports under `dataset/evidence/`.

## Input

Each row in `deletion_requests.csv` is one case:

```text
case_id,email,requested_at,reported_status,reported_at,audit_at,request_text
```

`reported_status` may be `COMPLETE`, `PARTIALLY_COMPLETE`, or `NOT_STARTED`. It is a claim from the existing deletion workflow, not proof of the true state.

## Required Output

Produce exactly one row in `output.csv` for every case in `deletion_requests.csv`:

```text
case_id,deletion_status,steps_json
```

| Column | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| `case_id` | Case being audited |
| `deletion_status` | `COMPLETE`, `INCOMPLETE`, or `NOT_APPLICABLE` |
| `steps_json` | Ordered JSON array of findings, remediation actions, and verification checks |

The final file must contain exactly 518 data rows, sorted by `case_id`.

Each object in `steps_json` must contain:

```json
{
  "step_no": 1,
  "phase": "FINDING",
  "system_id": "account_service",
  "resource_id": "account_005",
  "operation": "REPORT_REMAINING_RECORD",
  "state": "PRESENT",
  "evidence_id": "evidence_01"
}
```

`phase` must be `FINDING`, `REMEDIATION`, or `VERIFICATION`. Step numbers start at `1` and increase consecutively. Array order is significant.

For `COMPLETE`, include only verification steps proving the current state. For `INCOMPLETE`, include findings, the complete remediation plan, and the checks that must pass afterward. For `NOT_APPLICABLE`, use an empty JSON array (`[]`) because no deletion or deletion verification is authorized.

A non-deletion row therefore has this form:

```csv
case_000,NOT_APPLICABLE,[]
```

The grader parses `steps_json` as JSON. JSON object-key order and whitespace are ignored; array order is not.

## Allowed Operations

| Phase | Operation | State | Meaning |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `FINDING` | `REPORT_REMAINING_RECORD` | `PRESENT` | A record that should have been deleted still exists. |
| `FINDING` | `REPORT_EXPOSED_PII` | `PRESENT` | A record may remain, but it still contains identifiers that should have been redacted or anonymized. |
| `FINDING` | `REPORT_ACTIVE_DATA_FLOW` | `ACTIVE` | An active process can copy or recreate the user's data. |
| `FINDING` | `REPORT_RESTORE_RISK` | `ACTIVE` | A backup or restore path can bring deleted data back. |
| `REMEDIATION` | `DISABLE_DATA_FLOW` | `DISABLED` | Stop the process that can copy or recreate the data. |
| `REMEDIATION` | `BLOCK_RESTORE` | `BLOCKED` | Prevent the affected backup from restoring the user's data. |
| `REMEDIATION` | `DELETE_RECORD` | `ABSENT` | Remove the entire record. |
| `REMEDIATION` | `REDACT_RECORD` | `REDACTED` | Keep the record but remove the identifying fields required by policy. |
| `REMEDIATION` | `ANONYMIZE_RECORD` | `ANONYMIZED` | Keep the record after irreversibly unlinking it from the user. |
| `VERIFICATION` | `VERIFY_RECORD_ABSENT` | `ABSENT` | Confirm that a record no longer exists. |
| `VERIFICATION` | `VERIFY_RECORD_PRESENT` | `PRESENT` | Confirm that a policy-required retained record still exists. |
| `VERIFICATION` | `VERIFY_RECORD_REDACTED` | `REDACTED` | Confirm that required identifying fields have been removed. |
| `VERIFICATION` | `VERIFY_RECORD_ANONYMIZED` | `ANONYMIZED` | Confirm that the record can no longer be linked to the user. |
| `VERIFICATION` | `VERIFY_DATA_FLOW_DISABLED` | `DISABLED` | Confirm that the relevant data flow is inactive. |
| `VERIFICATION` | `VERIFY_RESTORE_BLOCKED` | `BLOCKED` | Confirm that the affected data cannot be restored. |

## Rules

- Resolve the user’s current and historical identifiers.
- Determine the resource state at `audit_at` using the available evidence. Reported workflow statuses and operation results are claims.
- Apply `deletion_rules.csv` to every relevant record, including account tombstones, active data flows, and restore risks.
- Order steps by phase: findings, remediation, then verification. Within each phase, follow the operation order in the table above, followed by `system_id` and `resource_id` in ascending order.
- Findings and verification steps for `COMPLETE` cases must cite a `file_id`, `activity_id`, or `audit_id`. Proposed remediation and future verification steps for `INCOMPLETE` cases use an empty `evidence_id`.

## Evaluation

`output.csv` will be compared with hidden canonical answers. Scoring will consider the correctness of `deletion_status`, identity resolution, discovered records and risks, cited evidence, policy decisions, and the completeness and order of `steps_json`.

Your `code.zip` must include `evaluation/evaluation_report.md` containing:

- case-level status accuracy;
- step-level and field-level accuracy;
- sequence accuracy;
- at least three additional evaluation cases created by your team; and
- failures found and improvements made.

| `output.csv` | One answer row for every request |
| `chat_transcript` | Transcript showing how the system was developed or used |
| AI interview | Explanation of the approach and evaluation |

HackerRank Orchestrate (October 2026) — Ghost Data Still Exists

## 0. TLDR For The Agent

2. Check the log file path in §2.

## 1. What This Repo Is

This is a starter repo for the **HackerRank Orchestrate** challenge: **Ghost Data Still Exists**.

Participants must build an AI-powered deletion-audit agent. For every request in `dataset/deletion_requests.csv`, the agent first determines whether the person actually requested deletion. For an authorized deletion, it reconstructs the person's current and historical identifiers, searches global activity and evidence, applies the supplied deletion policy, and decides whether the deletion is complete.

The dataset models a commerce platform where a workflow can report **Complete** while personal data remains in databases, replicas, logs, support attachments, exports, search indexes, analytics records, archives, or backups. Active synchronization and restore processes can also recreate data that was deleted from its original source. Reported statuses and activity results are claims; the agent must establish the state at each case's `audit_at` time from the supplied evidence.

When deletion is incomplete, the agent reports the remaining records and recreation risks, produces a safe ordered remediation plan, and states the verification checks that must pass afterward. When deletion is complete, it returns the evidence-backed verification checks that prove the required state. It must preserve records required by policy and correctly handle account tombstones whose stable internal user reference remains while prohibited direct identifiers are removed.

The final submission must produce `output.csv` with:

```text
case_id,deletion_status,steps_json
```

Read `problem_statement.md` for the full participant-facing specification.

### 5.2 Per-Turn Entry

Append after every user message you respond to:

```text
## [ISO-8601 TIMESTAMP] <short title, max 80 chars>

User Prompt (verbatim, secrets redacted):
<exact user message, with secrets replaced by [REDACTED]>

Agent Response Summary:
<2-5 sentences: what was done, why, and any important decision>

Actions:
* <file edited / command run / tool invoked>

Context:
tool=<exact_harness_or_coding_agent_name>
model=<exact_runtime_model_name>
branch=<git_branch_or_unknown>
repo_root=<absolute_path>
worktree=<worktree_path_or_main>
parent_agent=<parent_name_or_none>
```

**Mandatory tool-name rule:** Every `SESSION START` and per-turn log entry must contain one non-empty `tool=` line with the exact name of the coding harness or agent writing the entry. Replace the template value before writing the log. The entry is invalid if `tool=` is missing, blank, still contains a placeholder, uses a generic label such as `AI`, contains only a model name, or names a different harness. Before responding, verify the value against the harness identity provided by the current runtime and re-read the appended entry to confirm it matches. Never guess the tool name. Correct any mismatch before responding to the user.

**Mandatory model-name rule:** Every `SESSION START` and per-turn log entry must contain one non-empty `model=` line with the exact model identifier used for that turn. Always make sure that model name is correct & not made up. `model=unknown`, a model family, a generic label, an inferred name, and a template placeholder are invalid. Obtain the exact identifier from the runtime, session metadata, harness configuration, or another authoritative environment source before writing the entry. Never guess the model name. The model name does not replace the `tool=` value because the coding harness and the model are separate fields.

**Required harness-and-model check:** Both `tool=` and `model=` must always be present in every `SESSION START` and per-turn entry. An entry is invalid if either field is missing, blank, unknown, generic, inferred, or still contains a template placeholder. Before completing a response, re-read the newly appended log entry and confirm that it contains exactly one populated `tool=` line and exactly one populated `model=` line with authoritative values. If either is absent or invalid, determine the correct value and correct the entry before responding.

- Sub-agents must log their own entries using the same file.
- Set `parent_agent=` to the parent agent's name.
- Worktrees use the same shared log file, not a per-worktree copy.

- API keys, tokens, session cookies, OAuth codes, credentials, or private keys.
- Sensitive configuration or real personal data.

## 6. Project Contract

### 6.1 Dataset Contract

Participant-facing files are inside `dataset/`.

```text
dataset/
├── deletion_requests.csv
├── sample_deletion_requests.csv
├── sample_output.csv
├── user_identities.csv
├── user_audit_log.csv
├── system_activity.csv
├── deletion_rules.csv
├── evidence_files.csv
├── output.csv
└── evidence/
    ├── databases/
    ├── documents/
    ├── images/
    ├── logs/
    ├── records/
```

- `deletion_requests.csv` contains 518 evaluation requests. Produce exactly one output row for every `case_id` in this file.
- `sample_deletion_requests.csv` contains 32 public requests. `sample_output.csv` contains their correct outputs and demonstrates the required decision and serialization format.
- `user_identities.csv` contains the current identity snapshot for each user, including the stable `user_id`, name, email, phone, email hash, and snapshot update time.
- `user_audit_log.csv` records historical changes to identity fields. Use it with the current snapshot to recover identifiers that may still appear in older systems and evidence.
- `system_activity.csv` is a global stream of deletion, synchronization, export, backup, restore, indexing, and audit events. Its rows may describe unrelated people. Determine resource state from the available evidence rather than trusting workflow or operation results alone.
- `deletion_rules.csv` defines the required action for each data category and condition. Actions include deleting the whole record, redacting prohibited identifiers, anonymizing the record, retaining it, or preserving an account tombstone with only the permitted stable reference.
- `evidence_files.csv` is the manifest for evidence under `dataset/evidence/`. It maps each `file_id` to its path, source origin, and capture time. The manifest does not identify which case or user a file concerns; the solution must connect evidence through its contents and identifiers.
- `output.csv` is the blank prediction template. The final completed `output.csv` must be written at the repository root for submission.
- Evidence may be provided as PDF documents, application screenshots, logs, JSON, plain-text records, and SQLite-compatible SQL snapshot exports. SQL files are readable text and may be executed with SQLite.

Only `deletion_requests.csv` requires predictions. Other participant-facing files provide identity, policy, activity, and evidence context. Global data can contain decoys and unrelated records.

Organizer-only files live outside `dataset/` and must never be used for predictions, prompt examples, hardcoded labels, or evaluation-time shortcuts.

### 6.2 Input And Time Contract

Each request has these fields:

```text
case_id,email,requested_at,reported_status,reported_at,audit_at,request_text
```

- `case_id` uniquely identifies the request and its output row.
- `email` is the identity supplied with the request. It may be a current or historical email address.
- `requested_at` is when the request was submitted.
- `reported_status` is the deletion workflow's claim: `COMPLETE`, `PARTIALLY_COMPLETE`, or `NOT_STARTED`.
- `reported_at` is when that claim was reported.
- `audit_at` is the cutoff for the required answer.
- `request_text` contains the person's request and may be written in different languages. Determine whether it actually authorizes deletion.

Use only information available at or before `audit_at` when deciding the case. Evidence created later must not change the answer for that case.

### 6.3 Required Output

The solution must write `output.csv` with these exact columns, in this order:

```text
case_id,deletion_status,steps_json
```

Requirements:

- Produce exactly 518 data rows: one for every `case_id` in `dataset/deletion_requests.csv`.
- Sort rows by `case_id`.
- Set `deletion_status` to `COMPLETE`, `INCOMPLETE`, or `NOT_APPLICABLE`.
- Use `NOT_APPLICABLE` with `steps_json=[]` when the request does not authorize deletion.
- Encode `steps_json` as a valid JSON array. The grader ignores JSON object-key order and whitespace but preserves array order.
- Number steps consecutively from `1`.
- Order phases as `FINDING`, then `REMEDIATION`, then `VERIFICATION`.

Each step contains exactly these fields:

```json
{
  "step_no": 1,
  "phase": "FINDING",
  "system_id": "account_service",
  "resource_id": "account_005",
  "operation": "REPORT_REMAINING_RECORD",
  "state": "PRESENT",
  "evidence_id": "evidence_01"
}
```

For a `COMPLETE` case, include only evidence-backed verification steps proving the state at `audit_at`. For an `INCOMPLETE` case, include the findings, the complete ordered remediation plan, and the checks that must pass after remediation. Findings cite participant-visible evidence. Proposed remediation and future verification steps use an empty `evidence_id`.

### 6.4 Allowed Step Operations

Findings:

| Operation | State | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `REPORT_REMAINING_RECORD` | `PRESENT` | A record that should have been deleted still exists. |
| `REPORT_EXPOSED_PII` | `PRESENT` | A record may remain, but identifiers that policy requires to be redacted or anonymized are still exposed. |
| `REPORT_ACTIVE_DATA_FLOW` | `ACTIVE` | An active process can copy or recreate the user's data. |
| `REPORT_RESTORE_RISK` | `ACTIVE` | A backup or restore path can bring deleted data back. |

Remediation:

| Operation | State | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `DISABLE_DATA_FLOW` | `DISABLED` | Stop a process that can copy or recreate the data. |
| `BLOCK_RESTORE` | `BLOCKED` | Prevent the affected backup from restoring the user's data. |
| `DELETE_RECORD` | `ABSENT` | Remove the entire record. |
| `REDACT_RECORD` | `REDACTED` | Keep the record but remove the identifying fields required by policy. |
| `ANONYMIZE_RECORD` | `ANONYMIZED` | Keep the record after irreversibly unlinking it from the user. |

Verification:

| Operation | State | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `VERIFY_RECORD_ABSENT` | `ABSENT` | Confirm that a record no longer exists. |
| `VERIFY_RECORD_PRESENT` | `PRESENT` | Confirm that a policy-required retained record still exists. |
| `VERIFY_RECORD_REDACTED` | `REDACTED` | Confirm that required identifying fields were removed. |
| `VERIFY_RECORD_ANONYMIZED` | `ANONYMIZED` | Confirm that the record can no longer be linked to the user. |
| `VERIFY_DATA_FLOW_DISABLED` | `DISABLED` | Confirm that the relevant data flow is inactive. |
| `VERIFY_RESTORE_BLOCKED` | `BLOCKED` | Confirm that the affected data cannot be restored. |

Do not invent additional phases, operations, or states.

### 6.5 Deletion Decision Rules

- First determine whether `request_text` authorizes deletion. A request for access, correction, export, marketing opt-out, or another non-deletion action is `NOT_APPLICABLE`, even if other fields resemble a deletion case.
- Resolve both current and historical identifiers for the subject. The request email may match either form.
- Reconstruct the true state at `audit_at`. Ignore later evidence and do not accept `reported_status` or `reported_result` as proof.
- Apply `deletion_rules.csv` to every relevant record. Do not delete data that policy requires to be retained, redacted, anonymized, or preserved as a tombstone.
- For an `ACCOUNT_TOMBSTONE`, preserve the stable subject reference and remove the name, email, phone, email hash, and any other direct identifiers required by the applicable `REDACT` rule.
- A deletion is `COMPLETE` only when every relevant copy has the required treatment, every required retained record remains available, and no active sync, import, or restore path can recreate prohibited data.
- For an incomplete case, report every relevant policy violation and recreation risk supported by participant-visible evidence.
- Within remediation, disable data flows and block unsafe restores before changing dependent records. Process downstream copies before their source records when the dependency requires it.
- Verification must cover the required final state for each remediated record or process.

### 6.6 Evidence Rules

- Treat activity logs, workflow statuses, and system messages as claims unless the available evidence establishes the resulting state.
- Use `evidence_files.csv` to locate files, then inspect their contents. Do not infer case membership from the manifest alone.
- Match evidence through current identifiers, historical identifiers, hashes, resource IDs, system IDs, timestamps, and other grounded links.
- Images and PDFs are ordinary business artifacts such as invoices, support records, handover documents, and internal product screens. Extract the facts they contain; do not expect an explicit deletion verdict.
- SQL evidence can contain primary, regional, reporting, or failover replicas. Compare the relevant tables and snapshot time when investigating replication drift.
- A finding must use the participant-visible `file_id`, `activity_id`, or `audit_id` that proves it.
- A verification step for a `COMPLETE` case must cite the evidence that proves the current compliant state.
- Remediation steps and post-remediation verification steps for an `INCOMPLETE` case describe future work and therefore use `evidence_id` as an empty string.
- Never cite organizer-only truth or an evidence item captured after the case's `audit_at`.

### 6.7 Constraints That Make The Submission Evaluable

- Be runnable from the terminal.
- Read the provided files from `dataset/`.
- Write the completed `output.csv` at the repository root.
- Do not use organizer-only files, hardcoded golden labels, or case-family shortcuts.
- Keep behavior deterministic where possible, including ordering and JSON serialization.
- Read secrets from environment variables only.
- Include clear setup and run instructions in the submitted `README.md`.
- Ensure a clean run can process all 518 cases and produce exactly one valid row per case.

### 6.8 Evaluation And Token Usage

Build an AI agent that audits whether a user's personal data was actually deleted.

Build your solution in `code/main.py`, or use another language and document its setup and run command clearly in your README.

Your solution must:

- Read the input files from `dataset/`

Run the starter Python entry point with:

```bash
python3 code/main.py
```

The blank template at `dataset/output.csv` is provided as a reference. Your final generated file for submission must be the root-level `output.csv`.


## Dataset

All participant-facing files are in `dataset/`:

| File | Contents |
| --- | --- |
| `deletion_requests.csv` | 518 requests |
| `sample_deletion_requests.csv` | 32 sample requests |
| `sample_output.csv` | Correct sample answers |
| `user_identities.csv` | Current user identities |
| `user_audit_log.csv` | Historical identity changes |
| `system_activity.csv` | Activity across services and data processes |
| `deletion_rules.csv` | Required treatment for each data category |
| `evidence_files.csv` | Manifest of supplied evidence files |
| `output.csv` | Blank output template |

Evidence is stored under `dataset/evidence/` and may include PDFs, images, logs, JSON, plain text, or SQL snapshot exports. SQL captures may contain a primary database, regional replicas, a reporting replica, or a failover replica so replication drift can be investigated directly. The `.sql` files are readable as text and executable with SQLite.

Only `deletion_requests.csv` requires answers. The other files provide evidence and context.

---

## Output

Create `output.csv` with these columns in this exact order:

```text
case_id,deletion_status,steps_json
```

Produce exactly one row per request. `deletion_status` is `COMPLETE`, `INCOMPLETE`, or `NOT_APPLICABLE`. `steps_json` is an ordered JSON array containing findings, remediation actions, and verification checks. Use an empty array (`[]`) for `NOT_APPLICABLE`. Evidence references may be a `file_id`, `activity_id`, or `audit_id`. Determine the resource state at `audit_at` from the available evidence; reported workflow statuses and operation results are claims. Use `dataset/sample_output.csv` as the format reference.

The final file must contain 518 data rows sorted by `case_id` (plus the header row). The grader parses `steps_json` as JSON, so object-key order and whitespace do not matter; array order does. Order findings, remediation, and verification in that phase order. Within a phase, follow the operation order in the problem statement, then sort by `system_id` and `resource_id`.

---

## Evaluation

`output.csv` will be compared with hidden canonical answers. Scoring considers:

- deletion assessment accuracy;
- current and historical identity resolution;
- discovery of remaining data and recreation risks;
- correct policy application and evidence references; and
- completeness and ordering of remediation and verification steps.

Your `code.zip` must include:

```text
code/evaluation/evaluation_report.md
code/evaluation/usage_report.md
```

- `evaluation_report.md` must cover case, row, field, and sequence accuracy, at least three additional evaluation cases created by you, failures found, and improvements made.
- `usage_report.md` must cover the final full-dataset run, including model providers and names, model calls, input and output tokens, total and average tokens per request, and estimated total and per-request cost. Include per-model and overall totals when using multiple models.

**Important:** Place `evaluation_report.md` and `usage_report.md` inside `code/evaluation/` (matching `code/evaluation/main.py` in this starter layout), write your complete evaluation metrics, custom evaluation test cases, and token/cost breakdowns directly inside those Markdown files, and keep prompts in source code or Markdown files. Automated code evaluation ignores raw data files (`.csv`, `.tsv`, `.json`, `.jsonl`, `.sql`, `.log`), so content stored only in those formats will not be visible to the code judge or AI interviewer.

---

## Chat Transcript

This repository includes [`AGENTS.md`](./AGENTS.md), which instructs compatible AI coding tools to append conversation summaries to `log.txt` in the repository root:

| Platform | Path |
| --- | --- |
| macOS / Linux | `<repo root>/log.txt` |
| Windows | `<repo root>\log.txt` |

- Always launch your AI coding agent from the repository root (or explicitly ask it to read `./AGENTS.md` first) so every turn is logged to `log.txt` with the required `User Prompt`, `Agent Response Summary`, `Actions`, and `Context` sections.
- Upload `log.txt` directly as your `chat_transcript` submission on HackerRank.
- Do not include API keys, tokens, credentials, or secrets in prompts, code, configuration, or `log.txt`.

---

## Packaging `code.zip` and Submission

| Item | Description |
| --- | --- |
| `code.zip` | Runnable solution, prompts, configuration, `README.md`, and `evaluation/` reports |
| `output.csv` | One answer row for each of the 518 requests in `dataset/deletion_requests.csv` |
| `chat_transcript` | The root `log.txt` file generated by your coding agent |
| AI interview | Live explanation of your architecture, approach, and evaluation |

You may use any programming language, model, framework, or retrieval approach. Include clear setup and execution instructions in `README.md` inside `code.zip`.

To package `code.zip` from the repository root without bundling the dataset:

**macOS / Linux:**

```bash
zip -r code.zip README.md code -x "*/__pycache__/*" "*/.venv/*" "*/venv/*" "*/node_modules/*" "*/.git/*" "*/.DS_Store"
```

**Windows (PowerShell):**

```powershell
Compress-Archive -Path README.md, code -DestinationPath code.zip -Force
```

When packaging `code.zip`:

- Keep your evaluation reports in `code/evaluation/` so they are bundled automatically with `code` (or add `evaluation` to the archive command above if you placed them in a top-level `evaluation/` directory).
- Do **not** include `dataset/`, virtual environments (`.venv`, `venv`, `node_modules`), or build caches (`__pycache__`). On Windows, keep `.venv` at the repository root (outside `code/`) and remove any `code/__pycache__` folders before running `Compress-Archive`.
- Do **not** include nested archives (`.zip`, `.tar`, `.gz`, `.tgz`, `.7z`, `.rar`) inside `code.zip` — submissions containing nested archive files will be rejected.

Before submitting, confirm that:

- `output.csv` has the exact header `case_id,deletion_status,steps_json` and contains all 518 data rows sorted by `case_id`;
- every `steps_json` value is a valid JSON array with consecutive step numbers starting at `1`;
- findings cite valid evidence IDs, and `COMPLETE` verification steps cite evidence proving the current state;
- proposed remediation and future verification steps use an empty `evidence_id` (`""`);
- `code.zip` includes your runnable code, `README.md`, `code/evaluation/evaluation_report.md`, and `code/evaluation/usage_report.md`;
- `log.txt` follows the `AGENTS.md` turn format and is uploaded to the `chat_transcript` slot; and
- no API keys, credentials, real personal data, or sensitive configuration values are included.
-----
# Agent Trace Instructions

Please submit the complete agent trace for the work you did on this challenge as a **ZIP file**. A trace helps us understand the prompts, model responses, tool calls, and work performed during the session and improve our evaluation from the next time.

Include every session you used for this challenge, including sessions in which you planned, coded, debugged, or reviewed the solution. If you used more than one agent harness, include traces from each one. Keep the original files and folder structure where practical; do not convert JSONL, SQLite, or other structured logs into screenshots or summaries.

## Before you start

1. Finish the session(s) you intend to submit, then quit the app or CLI before copying its files. This helps ensure logs are written completely.
2. Create a temporary folder named `agent-trace` somewhere easy to find, such as your Desktop. Copy only the relevant trace files into it. Keep each tool in its own subfolder, for example `agent-trace/claude-code/` and `agent-trace/codex/`.
3. Include the session files for this challenge, not your entire home directory or application-data folder.
4. Review the staged files before sharing. Traces can contain source code, local file paths, prompts, tool arguments and output, screenshots, and other private information. Remove or redact secrets (API keys, tokens, passwords, cookies, private keys), personal information, and material you are not allowed to share. Do not include authentication files, settings, or credentials.
5. Zip the `agent-trace` folder using your operating system’s built-in archive tool. Name the result `agent-trace.zip`.

## Claude Code

### Where traces are stored

Claude Code stores session transcripts by project under:

- **macOS/Linux:** `~/.claude/projects/`
- **Windows:** `%USERPROFILE%\.claude\projects\`

Each project folder contains session files, commonly JSONL files named with a session ID. Find the folder corresponding to your challenge checkout and copy the session files you created for this challenge into `agent-trace/claude-code/`. The folder name may encode the project path, so check the session content and timestamps to identify the right files.

### Export a readable transcript

In each Claude Code session, run:

```text
/export
```

Save the exported transcript in `agent-trace/claude-code/`. `/export` produces a readable conversation export; include the original session JSONL files as well when available because they preserve the structured trace.

If you used Claude in the Claude web app or desktop chat rather than Claude Code, that is a different product history: use **Settings → Privacy → Export data** and follow the download instructions. Include only the relevant conversation export.

## OpenAI Codex

### Where traces are stored

Codex CLI and local Codex app sessions commonly store structured rollout files under:

- **macOS/Linux:** `~/.codex/sessions/`
- **Windows:** `%USERPROFILE%\.codex\sessions\`

The files are organized into date folders and are typically named `rollout-*.jsonl`. If you set `CODEX_HOME`, look under `$CODEX_HOME/sessions/` instead. Copy the rollout JSONL files for the challenge sessions into `agent-trace/codex/`. You can use their timestamps and session IDs to identify them. Include a matching `session_index.jsonl` only if useful for mapping session IDs to titles; review it for unrelated information first.

Do **not** copy `auth.json`, `config.toml`, credential stores, or the whole `.codex` directory.

If you used Codex only in a hosted/cloud interface and there is no local rollout file, use the interface’s conversation export or share/download option if available. If no transcript download is available, submit the conversation transcript in the format the interface provides and note that it was a hosted session with no local trace file.

## Google Antigravity

Antigravity writes a `transcript.jsonl` for each conversation under that conversation’s `brain` folder. The transcript includes agent activity; the same folder can also contain artifacts and screenshots associated with the conversation. Preserve the full relevant conversation folder when you need its artifacts to understand the trace.

Typical locations:

- **Antigravity app:** `~/.gemini/antigravity/brain/<conversation-id>/`
- **Antigravity CLI:** `~/.gemini/antigravity-cli/brain/<conversation-id>/`
- **Antigravity IDE:** `~/.gemini/antigravity-ide/brain/<conversation-id>/`
- **Windows:** look under `%USERPROFILE%\.gemini\antigravity\brain\`, `%USERPROFILE%\.gemini\antigravity-cli\brain\`, or `%USERPROFILE%\.gemini\antigravity-ide\brain\`, depending on the product used.

Within the relevant conversation folder, the transcript is at:

```text
.system_generated/logs/transcript.jsonl
```

Copy the complete `<conversation-id>` folder(s) for this challenge to `agent-trace/antigravity/`. Do not copy the entire `.gemini` directory: it may contain unrelated data. Antigravity CLI’s `last_conversations.json` is a session lookup cache, not the transcript itself.

## OpenCode

OpenCode stores session and message data in a local SQLite database. Its default application-data directory is:

- **macOS/Linux:** `~/.local/share/opencode/`
- **Windows:** `%USERPROFILE%\.local\share\opencode\`

The database is usually `opencode.db`. To find its active path (including custom XDG or `OPENCODE_DB` locations), run:

```sh
opencode db path
```

Prefer exporting each relevant session as JSON rather than copying the database. List sessions and export each challenge session:

```sh
opencode session list --format json
opencode session export <session-id> > <session-id>.json
```

To redact sensitive transcript and file data during export, add `--sanitize`:

```sh
opencode session export <session-id> --sanitize > <session-id>.json
```

Place the exported files in `agent-trace/opencode/`. If the CLI is unavailable and you need to provide the raw database instead, quit OpenCode first, copy only `opencode.db` into the staging folder, and inspect the copy for unrelated/private data. Never include `auth.json`, which can contain provider credentials. The default OpenCode data folder also contains logs and snapshots; those are not substitutes for the session exports.

## Other Harnesses

If you used an agent harness or coding tool that is not listed above (for example, Aider, Goose, RooCode, Cline, Devin, Factory, or JetBrains Junie):

1. **Finish and quit the session** so all logs and session files are completely written to disk.
2. **Create a tool subfolder** inside `agent-trace/` named after the harness (for example, `agent-trace/cursor/`, `agent-trace/windsurf/`, `agent-trace/copilot/`, `agent-trace/aider/`, or `agent-trace/goose/`).
3. **Export and copy the session traces for this challenge:**
   - Use the tool’s built-in session, chat, or trajectory export feature (UI or CLI) to save a readable or structured export into `agent-trace/<harness-name>/`.
   - Copy the raw structured trace files for this challenge from the tool’s local storage directory (such as `.jsonl`, `.json`, `.db`, or chat history files) into `agent-trace/<harness-name>/`, preserving original file formats and any subagent folders. Include only the sessions from this challenge.
4. **Redact secrets and exclude credentials:** Remove or redact any API keys, tokens, passwords, cookies, private keys, or personal data. Do **not** include authentication files, settings, or credential stores (such as `auth.json`, `config.json`, `mcp.json`, `mcp_config.json`, or `.env`).
5. **Package and upload:** Follow the steps below to create `agent-trace.zip` and upload it with your challenge submission.

## Create the ZIP

### macOS or Linux

From the directory containing `agent-trace/`:

```sh
zip -r agent-trace.zip agent-trace
```

### Windows PowerShell

From the directory containing `agent-trace/`:

```powershell
Compress-Archive -Path .\agent-trace -DestinationPath .\agent-trace.zip -Force
```

Open the ZIP once after creating it and confirm it contains the intended traces and no credentials or unrelated files. Submit `agent-trace.zip` with your challenge submission.

 "content": "\"\"\"Identity resolution: request e-mail -> stable user_id plus every identifier ever held.\n\nThe request e-mail may be a current or a historical address. History comes from\n`user_audit_log.csv` (field changes with timestamps). Ownership of an address is\ntemporal: an address belongs to the user who held it at the time of the request,\nso two users are never merged because one inherited a recycled address.\n\"\"\"\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nimport csv\nimport hashlib\nfrom dataclasses import dataclass, field\nfrom pathlib import Path\nfrom typing import Optional\n\nfrom .util import norm_email, norm_phone, parse_ts\n\n\n@dataclass\nclass User:\n    user_id: str\n    name: str\n    email: str\n    phone: str\n    email_hash: str\n    updated_at: str\n    # (email, valid_from, valid_to, audit_id that ended it or None)\n    email_history: list[tuple[str, str, str, Optional[str]]] = field(default_factory=list)\n    phones: set[str] = field(default_factory=set)\n    hashes: set[str] = field(default_factory=set)\n    audit_ids: list[str] = field(default_factory=list)\n\n    @property\n    def emails(self) -> set[str]:\n        return {e for e, *_ in self.email_history}\n\n    def identifiers(self) -> set[str]:\n        return {self.user_id} | self.emails | self.hashes\n\n\n@dataclass\nclass Resolution:\n    user: Optional[User]\n    via: str  # current | historical | none | ambiguous\n    matched_email: str\n    audit_ids: list[str] = field(default_factory=list)  # audit events that prove a historical match\n    candidates: list[str] = field(default_factory=list)\n\n\nclass IdentityIndex:\n    def __init__(self, dataset_dir: Path):\n        self.users: dict[str, User] = {}\n        self.owners: dict[str, list[tuple[str, str, str]]] = {}  # email -> [(user, from, to)]\n        self.phone_owners: dict[str, set[str]] = {}\n        with open(dataset_dir / \"user_identities.csv\", newline=\"\") as f:\n            for r in csv.DictReader(f):\n                u = User(r[\"user_id\"], r[\"name\"], norm_email(r[\"email\"]), r[\"phone\"], r[\"email_hash\"].lower(), r[\"updated_at\"])\n                self.users[u.user_id] = u\n        changes: dict[str, list[dict]] = {}\n        with open(dataset_dir / \"user_audit_log.csv\", newline=\"\") as f:\n            for r in csv.DictReader(f):\n                changes.setdefault(r[\"user_id\"], []).append(r)\n        for uid, u in self.users.items():\n            rows = sorted(changes.get(uid, []), key=lambda r: r[\"event_time\"])\n            emails = [r    elif r[\"field_name\"] == \"email_hash\":\n                    u.hashes.update({r[\"old_value\"].lower(), r[\"new_value\"].lower()})\n            start = \"0000-00-00T00:00:00Z\"\n            if emails:\n                prev = start\n                for r in emails:\n                    u.email_history.append((norm_email(r[\"old_value\"]), prev, r[\"event_time\"], r[\"audit_id\"]))\n                    prev = r[\"event_time\"]\n                u.email_history.append((u.email, prev, \"9999-12-31T23:59:59Z\", None))\n            else:\n                u.email_history.append((u.email, start, u.hashes.add(hashlib.sha256(e.encode()).hexdigest())\n            for p in u.phones:\n                self.phone_owners.setdefault(p, set()).add(uid)\n\n    def resolve(self, email: str, requested_at: str) -> Resolution:\n        e = norm_email(email)\n        owners = self.owners.get(e, [])\n        if not owners:\n            return Resolution(None, \"none\", e)\n        users = sorted({o[0] for o in owners})\n        if len(users) > 1:\n            # recycled address: pick the holder at request time if that is unique\n            holders = sorted({uid for uid, a, b in owners if a <= requested_at <= b})\n            if len(holders) != 1:\n                return Resolution(None, \"ambiguous\", e, candidates=users)\n            users = holders\n        u = self.users[users[0]]\n        via = \"current\" if u.email == e else \"historical\"\n        proof = [aid for (em, _, _, aid) in u.email_history if em == e and aid]\n        return Resolution(u, via, e, proof)\n"

 "file_path": "/Users/madhusudhan/Desktop/Work-Space/hackerrank-orchestrate-october26/code/ghost_audit/timeline.py",
 "content": "\"\"\"Timeline reconstruction: the state of every resource *as of audit_at*.\n\nRules\n  * Observations after `audit_at` are ignored entirely (later evidence must not change the answer).\n  * For each resource the latest observation at or before `audit_at` wins, so a record\n    that was deleted and later recreated (e.g. by a sync job) is reported as present,\n    and a record that was present and later observed gone is reported as absent.\n  * Observations that share the latest timestamp come from different copies of the same\n    resource (primary vs. replicas, several services). The copies are merged\n    pessimistically - if any copy still exposes the record, the resource is not compliant -\n    because replication drift is a real violation.\n\"\"\"\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nimport datetime as dt\nfrom dataclasses import dataclass, field\nfrom typing import Iterable\n\nfrom .models import Obs\nfrom .util import parse_ts\n\n# state keys that merge with OR (violation if any copy shows it)\nOR_KEYS = (\"present\", \"exposed\", \"attributable\", \"enabled\", \"restore_allowed\")\n\n\n@dataclass\nclass ResState:\n    system: str\n    rid: str\n    kind: str\n    t: str\n    state: dict\n    evidence_id: str\n    obs: list[Obs] = field(default_factory=list)\n    sources: list[str] = field(default_factory=list)\n\n    @property\n    def key(self) -> tuple[str, str]:\n        return (self.system, self.rid)\n\n\ndef _fnum(fid: str) -> int:\n    try:\n        return int(fid.split(\"_\")[1])\n    except (IndexError, ValueError):\n        return 10**9\n\n\ndef violating(kind: str, state: dict) -> bool:\n    if kind in (\"account\", \"search\", \"export\", \"marketing\", \"support_attachment\", \"cache\", \"email_delivery\",\n                \"secondary_identifier\"):\n        return bool(state.get(\"present\", True))\n    if kind == \"support_message\" or kind == \"tombstone\":\n        return bool(state.get(\"exposed\"))\n    if kind in (\"analytics\", \"telemetry\"):\n        return bool(state.get(\"attributable\"))\n    if kind == \"data_flow\":\n        return bool(state.get(\"enabled\"))\n    if kind == \"backup\":\n        return bool(state.get(\"restore_allowed\"))\n    return False\n\n\ndef reduce_states(observations: Iterable[Obs], audit_at: str, not_before: str = \"\") -> dict[tuple[str, str, str], ResState]:\n    groups: dict[tuple[str, str, str], list[Obs]] = {}\n    for o in observations:\n        if o.t > audit_at or (not_before and o.t < not_before):\n            continue\n        groups.setdefault((o.system, o.rid, o.kind), []).append(o)\n    out: dict[tuple[str, str, str], ResState] = {}\n    for key, lst in groups.items():\n        tmax = max(o.t for o in lst)\n        latest = [o for o in lst if o.t == tmax]\n        merged: dict = {}\n        for o in latest:\n            for k, v in o.state.items():\n                if k in OR_KEYS:\n                    merged[k] = bool(merged.get(k, False) or v)\n                else:\n                    merged.setdefault(k, v)\n        if \"redacted\" in merged:\n            merged[\"redacted\"] = all(o.state.get(\"redacted\", False) for o in latest if \"redacted\" in o.state) and not merged.get(\"exposed\")\n        viol = violating(key[2], merged)\n        # cite an observation that actually proves the reduced state\n        proving = [o for o in latest if violating(key[2], o.state) == viol] or latest\n        proving.sort(key=lambda o: (_fnum(o.file_id), o.source))\n        out[key] = ResState(key[0], key[1], key[2], tmax, merged, proving[0].file_id, lst,\n                            sorted({o.source for o in latest}))\n    return out\n"

 "content": "\"\"\"Link resources that carry no identifier to the case they belong to, via system_activity.\n\nWhy this exists: an account that has been deleted (HTTP 404 / NOT_FOUND) and an\nanalytics profile that has been anonymized no longer contain any identifier, so\nthe evidence files cannot say whose they are. The deletion worker, however, wrote\none `DELETE_RECORD` for the case's account and a short chain of policy actions\naround the time the workflow reported its status. We therefore join on activity:\n\n  * time window  - the account delete lands shortly *before* `reported_at`;\n  * report claim - the activity `reported_result` mirrors the workflow's\n                   `reported_status` (SUCCESS/PARTIAL/FAILED);\n  * chain phase  - actions of one case are emitted by the same worker run, so their\n                   second-of-minute offsets relative to the account delete are\n                   constant (mod 30 s). The offsets are *learned from cases whose\n                   resources are identity-linked*, not hard-coded.\n\nActivity results are treated as claims only; they are used for linking, never as\nproof of a resource's state.\n\"\"\"\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nimport csv\nimport datetime as dt\nfrom collections import Counter, defaultdict\nfrom dataclasses import dataclass\nfrom pathlib import Path\nfrom typing import Optional\n\nfrom .util import parse_ts\n\nSTATUS_RESULT = {\"COMPLETE\": \"SUCCESS\", \"PARTIALLY_COMPLETE\": \"PARTIAL\", \"NOT_STARTED\": \"FAILED\"}\nMOD = 30.0\n\n\n@dataclass\nclass Activity:\n    activity_id: str\n    t: str\n    dtv: dt.datetime\n    event_type: str\n    source: str\n    target: str\n    rid: str\n    result: str\n    details: str\n\n\ndef load_activities(path: Path) -> list[Activity]:\n    out = []\n    with open(path, newline=\"\") as f:\n        for r in csv.DictReader(f):\n            out.append(Activity(r[\"activity_id\"], r[\"event_time\"], parse_ts(r[\"event_time\"]), r[\"event_type\"],\n                                r[\"source_system\"], r[\"target_system\"], r[\"resource_id\"], r[\"reported_result\"], r[\"details\"]))\n    return out\n\n\nclass ActivityIndex:\n    def __init__(self, acts: list[Activity]):\n        self.acts = acts\n        self.by_rid: dict[str, list[Activity]] = defaultdict(list)\n        for a in acts:\n            self.by_rid[a.rid].append(a)\n        self.account_deletes = [a for a in acts if a.event_type == \"DELETE_RECORD\" and a.source == \"deletion_worker\"\n                                and a.target == \"account_service\"]\n        self.worker_events = [a for a in acts if a.source == \"deletion_worker\"]\n\n    def worker_events_for(self, rid: str) -> list[Activity]:\n        return [a for a in self.by_rid.get(rid, []) if a.source == \"deletion_worker\"]\n\n\ndef _circ(d: float) -> float:\n    d = d % MOD\n    return min(d, MOD - d)\n\n\nclass PhaseModel:\n    \"\"\"Second-of-minute offset (mod 30s) between the account delete and the other\n    worker actions of the same case; separate offsets before/after the report.\"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(self):\n        self.off = {\"pre\": 10.0, \"post\": 21.0}  # defaults, replaced by learning\n        self.tol = 2.0\n        self.learned: dict[str, tuple[float, int, int]] = {}\n\n    def side(self, ev: Activity, reported: dt.datetime) -> str:\n        return \"post\" if ev.dtv >= reported else \"pre\"\n\n    def learn(self, samples: list[tuple[str, float]]) -> None:\n        by: dict[str, list[float]] = defaultdict(list)\n        for side, diff in samples:\n            by[side].append(diff % MOD)\n        for side, vals in by.items():\n            if len(vals) < 5:\n                continue\n            bins = Counter(int(round(v)) % int(MOD) for v in vals)\n            peak = max(bins, key=bins.get)\n            near = [((v - peak + MOD / 2) % MOD) - MOD / 2 for v in vals if _circ(v - peak) <= 3]\n            center = (peak + sum(near) / len(near)) % MOD\n            self.off[side] = center\n            self.learned[side] = (center, len(near), len(vals))\n\n    def ok(self, account_ev: Activity, ev: Activity, reported: dt.datetime) -> bool:\n        side = self.side(ev, reported)\n        diff = (ev.dtv - account_ev.dtv).total_seconds() - self.off[side]\n        return _circ(diff) <= self.tol\n\n    def dist(self, account_ev: Activity, ev: Activity, reported: dt.datetime) -> float:\n        side = self.side(ev, reported)\n        return _circ((ev.dtv - account_ev.dtv).total_seconds() - self.off[side])\n"

 "content": "\"\"\"Step planner: turns policy treatments into the ordered findings / remediation /\nverification list required by the specification.\"\"\"\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nfrom .models import Step\n\nFINDING_ORDER = [\"REPORT_REMAINING_RECORD\", \"REPORT_EXPOSED_PII\", \"REPORT_ACTIVE_DATA_FLOW\", \"REPORT_RESTORE_RISK\"]\nREMEDIATION_ORDER = [\"DISABLE_DATA_FLOW\", \"BLOCK_RESTORE\", \"DELETE_RECORD\", \"REDACT_RECORD\", \"ANONYMIZE_RECORD\"]\nVERIFICATION_ORDER = [\"VERIFY_RECORD_ABSENT\", \"VERIFY_RECORD_PRESENT\", \"VERIFY_RECORD_REDACTED\",\n                      \"VERIFY_RECORD_ANONYMIZED\", \"VERIFY_DATA_FLOW_DISABLED\", \"VERIFY_RESTORE_BLOCKED\"]\nPHASE_ORDER = {\"FINDING\": FINDING_ORDER, \"REMEDIATION\": REMEDIATION_ORDER, \"VERIFICATION\": VERIFICATION_ORDER}\nPHASE_RANK = {\"FINDING\": 0, \"REMEDIATION\": 1, \"VERIFICATION\": 2}\n\nFINDING_STATE = {\"REPORT_REMAINING_RECORD\": \"PRESENT\", \"REPORT_EXPOSED_PII\": \"PRESENT\",\n                 \"REPORT_ACTIVE_DATA_FLOW\": \"ACTIVE\", \"REPORT_RESTORE_RISK\": \"ACTIVE\"}\n\n# Whether compliant non-core records (support/analytics/flow/backup/tombstone) are\n# verified in an INCOMPLETE case. The specification asks verification to \"cover the\n# required final state for each remediated record or process\", so they are not.\nINCLUDE_COMPLIANT_IN_INCOMPLETE = False\n\n\ndef sort_steps(steps: list[Step]) -> list[Step]:\n    def key(s: Step):\n        return (PHASE_RANK[s.phase], PHASE_ORDER[s.phase].index(s.operation), s.system_id, s.resource_id)\n    seen, out = set(), []\n    for s in sorted(steps, key=key):\n        k = (s.phase, s.operation, s.system_id, s.resource_id)\n        if k in seen:\n            continue\n        seen.add(k)\n        out.append(s)\n    return out\n\n\ndef plan_steps(w, items, case_file: str) -> list[Step]:\n    \"\"\"`items` = [(ResState, Treatment)]; `w` is the per-case work object.\"\"\"\n    steps: list[Step] = []\n    violations = [(st, t) for st, t in items if not t.compliant]\n    incomplete = bool(violations)\n    account_seen = any(st.kind == \"account\" for st, _ in items)\n\n    for st, t in violations:\n        steps.append(Step(\"FINDING\", st.system, st.rid, t.finding, FINDING_STATE[t.finding], st.evidence_id))\n        op, state = t.remediation\n        steps.append(Step(\"REMEDIATION\", st.system, st.rid, op, state, \"\"))\n        vop, vstate = t.verification\n        steps.append(Step(\"VERIFICATION\", st.system, st.rid, vop, vstate, \"\"))\n\n    # the account is always verified absent once an account id is known\n    if w.account_rid and not account_seen:\n        steps.append(Step(\"VERIFICATION\", \"account_service\", w.account_rid, \"VERIFY_RECORD_ABSENT\", \"ABSENT\",\n                          \"\" if incomplete else case_file))\n\n    for st, t in items:\n        if t.compliant and t.verification:\n            core = st.kind in (\"account\", \"audit\", \"legal_hold\")\n            if incomplete and not (core or INCLUDE_COMPLIANT_IN_INCOMPLETE):\n                continue\n            if st.kind in (\"search\", \"export\", \"support_attachment\", \"marketing\", \"cache\", \"email_delivery\",\n                           \"secondary_identifier\"):\n                continue  # an already-absent non-account copy is not a retained or remediated record\n            steps.append(Step(\"VERIFICATION\", st.system, st.rid, t.verification[0], t.verification[1],\n                              \"\" if incomplete else st.evidence_id))\n    return sort_steps(steps)\n"
}

 "command": "cd /Users/madhusudhan/Desktop/Work-Space/hackerrank-orchestrate-october26/code && python3 - <<'EOF'\np='ghost_audit/chain.py'\ns=open(p).read()\nstart=s.index('class PhaseModel:')\ns=s[:start]+'''class PhaseModel:\n    \"\"\"Likelihood model of the second-of-minute offset between a case's account delete\n    and its other worker actions. Learned from identity-anchored cases:\n      * pooled per side (before/after the report) modulo 30 s (robust, ample data);\n      * per (side, event type) modulo 60 s (the worker alternates a 30 s shift by slot,\n        so the exact cluster depends on the action type) when enough samples exist.\"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(self):\n        self.tol = 2.0\n        self.pool30: dict[str, list[float]] = {}\n        self.typed60: dict[tuple[str, str], list[float]] = {}\n        self.learned: dict = {}\n\n    @staticmethod\n    def side(ev: Activity, reported: dt.datetime) -> str:\n        return \"post\" if ev.dtv >= reported else \"pre\"\n\n    def learn(self, samples: list[tuple[str, str, float]]) -> None:\n        for side, etype, diff in samples:\n            self.pool30.setdefault(side, []).append(diff % MOD)\n            self.typed60.setdefault((side, etype), []).append(diff % 60.0)\n        self.learned = {\"pooled\": {k: len(v) for k, v in self.pool30.items()},\n                        \"typed\": {f\"{k[0]}:{k[1]}\": len(v) for k, v in self.typed60.items() if len(v) >= 10}}\n\n    @staticmethod\n    def _density(vals: list[float], x: float, modulus: float, width: float = 1.5) -> float:\n        n = len(vals)\n        hit = sum(1 for v in vals if min((v - x) % modulus, (x - v) % modulus) <= width)\n        return (hit + 0.5) / (n + 1.0) / (2 * width)  # per-second density, lightly smoothed\n\n    def llr(self, account_ev: Activity, ev: Activity, reported: dt.datetime) -> float:\n        \"\"\"log-likelihood ratio that `ev` belongs to the same worker run as `account_ev`\n        versus an unrelated event (uniform phase).\"\"\"\n        import math\n        side = self.side(ev, reported)\n        diff = (ev.dtv - account_ev.dtv).total_seconds()\n        typed = self.typed60.get((side, ev.event_type), [])\n        if len(typed) >= 10:\n            return math.log(self._density(typed, diff % 60.0, 60.0) * 60.0)\n        pooled = self.pool30.get(side, [])\n        if len(pooled) >= 10:\n            return math.log(self._density(pooled, diff % MOD, MOD) * MOD)\n        return 0.0\n\n    def ok(self, account_ev: Activity, ev: Activity, reported: dt.datetime) -> bool:\n        return self.llr(account_ev, ev, reported) > 1.0\n'''\nopen(p,'w').write(s)\np='ghost_audit/auditor.py'\ns=open(p).read()\ns=s.replace('''                side = self.phase.side(ev, w.reported)\n                samples.append((side, (ev.dtv -  "content": "\"\"\"Document evidence (PDF text and OCR'd screenshots) -> observations.\n\nBusiness artifacts (customer statements, support attachments, admin-console\nscreenshots, retention journals) rarely contain a verdict; they show that a record\n*exists* and which identifiers it carries. the document, e.g. a statement number or file name - to a user;\n  2. the document mentions exactly one resource of the primary kind together with one of the\n     user's own identifiers (e-mail, user r\"(?:linked|confirmed)\\s+(?P<rid>[a-z_]+_\\w+)\\s+\\((?P<label>[^)]*)\\)\\s+(?:to|belongs only to)\\s+(?P<email>\\S+@\\S+?)\\s+and\\s+(?P<user>user_\\d+)\",\n    re.I)\n\n\n@dataclass\nclass re.sub(r\"\\b(account|search|support|analytics|export|backup|tombstone|marketing|object)[ ·]+(\\d{1,6})\\b\",\n               lambda m: f\"{m.group(1)}_{m.group(2)}\", text, flags=re.I)\n    t = re.sub(r\"\\b(audit)[ _]record[ _](\\d+)\\b\", r\"audit_record_\\2\", t, flags=re.I)\n    t = re.sub(r\"\\bdata[ _]flow[ _](\\d+)\\b\", r\"data_flow_\\1\", t, flags=re.I)\n    return t\n\n\ndef _kind_for(rid: str, text: str) -> Optional[str]:\n    p = prefix_of(rid)\n    low = text.lower()\n    if p == \"account\":\n        return \"account\"\n    if p == \"search\":\n        return \"search\"\n    if p == \"analytics\":\n        return \"analytics\"\n    if p == \"export\":\n        return \"export\"\n    if p == \"data_flow\":\n        return \"data_flow\"\n    if p == \"backup\":\n        return \"backup\"\n    if p == \"audit_record\":\n        return \"audit\"\n    if p == \"tombstone\":\n        return \"tombstone\"\n    if p == \"marketing\":\n        return \"marketing\"\n    if p == \"object\":\n        return \"support_attachment\"\n    if p == \"legal_hold\":\n        return \"legal_hold\"\n    if p == \"support\":\n        if \"attachment\" in low or \"uploader\" in low or \"file delivery_\" in low or \".pdf\" in low:\n            return \"support_attachment\"\n        return \"support_message\"\n    return None\n\n\ndef _state_for(kind: str, text: str, ids_present: bool) -> dict:\n    low = text.lower()\n    if kind == \"data_flow\":\n        off = any(w in low for w in (\"disabled\", \"paused\", \"stopped\", \"inactive\"))\n        return {\"enabled\": not off}\n    if kind == \"backup\":\n        blocked = any(w in low for w in (\"restore blocked\", \"restore denied\", \"restore_allowed=false\", \"blocked\"))\n        return {\"restore_allowed\": not blocked}\n    if kind == \"analytics\":\n        return {\"attributable\": ids_present}\n    if kind in (\"tombstone\", \"support_message\"):\n        return {\"present\": True, \"exposed\": ids_present, \"redacted\": not ids_present}\n    return {\"present\": True}\n\n\ndef docs_to_obs(docs: Iterable[DocText], catalog: list[CatalogLink]) -> list[Obs]:\n    by_rid: dict[str, list[CatalogLink]] = {}\n    by_label: dict[str, list[CatalogLink]] = {}\n    for c in catalog:\n        by_rid.setdefault(c.rid, []).append(c)\n        if c.label and c.label != c.rid:\n            by_label.setdefault(c.label, []).append(c)\n    out: list[Obs] = []\n    for d in docs:\n        text = _normalize(d.text)\n        rids = list(dict.fromkeys(RID_RE.findall(text)))\n        # catalog binding through printed labels (statement numbers, file names)\n        label_hits = [(lbl, links) for lbl, links in by_label.items() if lbl.lower() in text.lower()]\n        bound: dict[str, primary = [r for r in rids if prefix_of(r) in (\"support\", \"account\", \"search\", \"export\", \"backup\", \"tombstone\",\n                                                              \"analytics\", \"data_flow\", \"marketing\", \"object\")]\n            if len(primary) == 1:\n                bound[primary[0]] = doc_ids\n        for rid, ids in bound.items():\n            kind = Proximity matches (worker event logged within\n        minutes of the report) come next. The rest is a global assignment problem: edge weight =\n        phase log-likelihood of the case's anchor events against the candidate account delete (+ the\n        best anonymized-analytics phase match), solved optimally per connected component so a\n        coincidental phase match cannot steal an account from the case it truly belongs to.\"\"\"\n        claimed = {w.account_rid for w in works if w.account_rid}\n        free = [a for a in self.act.account_deletes if a.rid not in claimed]\n        pending = [w for w in works if w.deletion and w.res and w.res.user and not w.account_rid]\n        for w in list(pending):\n            R = w.reported\n            near = sorted((a for a in free if R - Implements the classic O(n^3) potentials method on a\n    square matrix padded with forbidden/dummy cells.\n    \"\"\"\n    n_r = len(weights)\n    n_c = len(weights[0]) if weights else 0\n    n = max(n_r, n_c)\n    if n == 0:\n        return []\n    # cost to minimize: matching a real pair earns BIG + weight; padded/forbidden cells cost 0 reward.\n    cost = [[0.0] * n for _ in range(n)]\n    for i in range(n_r):\n        for j in range(n_c):\n            w = weights[i][j]\n            if w is not None:\n                cost[i][j] = -(BIG + w)\n    INF = float(\"inf\")\n    u = [0.0] * (n + 1)\n    v = [0.0] * (n + 1)\n    p = [0] * (n + 1)\n    way = [0] * (n + 1)\n    for i in range(1, n + 1):\n        p[0] = i\n        j0 = 0\n        minv = [INF] * (n + 1)\n        used = [False] * (n + 1)\n        while True:\n            used[j0] = True\n            i0, delta, j1 = p[j0], INF, 0\n            for j in range(1, n + 1):\n                if not used[j]:\n                    cur = cost[i0 - 1][j - 1] - u[i0] - v[j]\n                    if cur < minv[j]:\n                        minv[j], way[j] = cur, j0\n                    if minv[j] < delta:\n                        delta, j1 = minv[j], j\n            for j in range(n + 1):\n                if used[j]:\n                    u[p[j]] += delta\n                    v[j] -= delta\n                else:\n                    minv[j] -= delta\n            j0 = j1\n            if p[j0] == 0:\n                break\n        while True:\n            j1 = way[j0]\n            p[j0] = p[j1]\n            j0 = j1\n            if j0 == 0:\n                break\n    out = []\n    for j in range(1, n + 1):\n        i = p[j]\n        if 1 <= i <= n_r and j <= n_c and weights[i - 1][j - 1] is not None:\n            out.append((i - 1, j - 1))\n    return out\n''')\nEOF\ncd .. && python3 -I code/main.py | tail -3;  "file_path": "/Users/madhusudhan/Desktop/Work-Space/hackerrank-orchestrate-october26/code/evaluation/main.py",
 "content": "\"\"\"Evaluation harness.\n\n    python3 code/evaluation/main.py            # score the public sample, run synthetic cases, write reports\n\nScores the pipeline against dataset/sample_output.csv (case, step, field and sequence level),\nruns the team-made SYNTHETIC scenarios in code/tests/scenarios.py, and renders\nevaluation_report.md and usage_report.md from the measured numbers (nothing is typed in by hand\nexcept the narrative sections kept in NARRATIVE below).\n\"\"\"\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nimport csv\nimport json\nimport sys\nimport time\nfrom collections import Counter, defaultdict\nfrom pathlib import Path\n\nHERE = Path(__file__).resolve().parent\nCODE = HERE.parent\nROOT = CODE.parent\nsys.path.insert(0, str(CODE))\nsys.path.insert(0, str(CODE / \"tests\"))\n\nfrom ghost_audit.auditor import Auditor  # field level: positionally aligned steps\n        for i in range(max(len(psteps), len(esteps))):\n            field_total += 1\n            if i < len(psteps) and i < len(esteps):\n                for k in FIELDS:\n                    field_hits[k] += psteps[i][k] == esteps[i][k]\n        # noqa: E731\n    return {\n        \"cases\": n, \"status_acc\": status_ok / n, \"row_exact\": rows_exact / n,\n        \"per_status\": {k: (v[0], v[1]) for k, v in sorted(per_status.items())},\n        \"steps_pred\": n_pred, \"steps_true\": n_true,\n        \"step_prec_strict\": prec, \"step_rec_strict\": rec, \"step_f1_strict\": f1(prec, rec),\n        \"step_prec_rel\": prec_r, \"step_rec_rel\": rec_r, \"step_f1_rel\": f1(prec_r, rec_r),\n        \"field_acc\": {k: field_hits[k] / field_total for k in FIELDS}, \"field_total\": field_total,\n        \"matched\": matched, \"state_acc_matched\": state_hits / matched if matched else 1.0,\n        \"evidence_acc_matched\": ev_hits / matched if matched else 1.0,\n        \"seq_exact\": seq_exact / n, \"seq_given_same_steps\": (seq_exact / seq_sets_equal) if seq_sets_equal else 1.0,\n        \"seq_lcs\": lcs_sum / n,\n    }\n\n\ndef diffs(pred, exp, cases):\n    out = []\n    for c in cases:\n        ps, psteps = pred[c]\n        es, esteps = exp[c]\n        key = lambda s: (s[\"phase\"], s[\"system_id\"], s[\"resource_id\"], s[\"operation\"], s[\"evidence_id\"])  # noqa: E731\n        P, E = {key(s) for s in psteps}, {key(s) for s in esteps}\n        if ps != es or P != E or [key(s) for s in psteps] != [key(s) for s in esteps]:\n            out.append((c, ps, es, sorted(E - P), sorted(P - E)))\n    return out\n\n\ndef pct(x: float) -> str:\n    return f\"{100 * x:.1f}%\"\n\n\nNARRATIVE_IMPROVEMENTS = \"\"\"\\\n| # The pipeline cannot emit ids it cannot observe, so those three sample rows can never match exactly. The same limitation applies to 91 of the 518 hidden-set predictions that contain no `security_service` verification step (no audit record for the user is present at or before `audit_at`).\n* **Chain linking is statistical for unanchored cases.** A completed case that consists only of an absent account, an audit record and an anonymized analytics profile has no identity-bearing resource to anchor on; its account is chosen by the learned phase model plus the optimal assignment. 21 of the 518 requests are flagged \"among N equally plausible deletion events\" in the run notes and may be wrong.\n* **Hand-built scenarios.** The twelve screenshot/PDF cases rely on OCR (Apple Vision, cached) and on catalog links; the correct answers for their account ids and secondary artefacts cannot be validated beyond the ten public hand-built samples.\n* **Heuristic intent classifier.** Language coverage is en/es/pt/fr/ja/ko plus explicit negations; it matches all 32 public requests and isolates 10 non-deletion requests in the 518, but an unseen phrasing could be misclassified.
