An LLM would only help for\n(1) free-text intent in languages beyond the six covered, (2) reading screenshots on machines without an OCR engine, and\n(3) explaining findings to a human. If added, calls would be cached by content hash, constrained to a JSON schema, validated by\n`ghost_audit/output.py::validate_output`, and their provider/model/tokens/cost logged in this file; keys would come from environment variables only.\n\"\"\"\n\n\ndef main() -> int:\n    ds = ROOT / \"dataset\"\n    if not (ds / \"sample_output.csv\").exists():\n        print(\"dataset/ not found - ## 1. How the pipeline was scored

The 32 rows of `dataset/sample_output.csv` are the only labelled data, so they are the only real accuracy measurement. Predictions for them are produced by the same deterministic code path as the 518 hidden requests (the 518 requests are loaded as linking context, never scored). Comparisons are exact: JSON is parsed, object-key order and whitespace are ignored, array order is not. No fuzzy matching hides errors.

## 4. Additional evaluation cases created by the team (SYNTHETIC)

These are fictional datasets (people, ids and timestamps invented) that follow the real file schemas. They are built by `code/tests/synth.py`, defined in `code/tests/scenarios.py`, and run through the production pipeline; they are **not** real dataset evidence and are not mixed into the numbers above.

| Scenario | Purpose | Result |
|---|---|---|
| `complete_verifiable` | Completed deletion: absent account, retained audit record, redacted support, anonymized analytics, disabled flow, blocked restore - every check cites evidence | PASS |
| `incomplete_flow_restore` | Remaining account/search/export, exposed support message, active data flow and restorable backup - ordered findings, remediation and verification | PASS |
| `non_deletion_and_retained` | Access request is NOT_APPLICABLE ([]); exposed tombstone is redacted not deleted; legal hold and audit record are verified present | PASS |
| `historical_email` | Request filed with a historical address resolves to the stable user via user_audit_log | PASS |
| `later_evidence_ignored` | A lookup after audit_at that shows the account recreated must not change the audit_at answer | PASS |

Result: 5/5 synthetic scenarios pass. In addition `pytest code/tests` runs 52 passed in 0.63s (identity resolution incl. a recycled address, multilingual intent, policy per rule, timeline/post-audit evidence, six log dialects + SQL replicas + JSON layouts, step ordering, validator errors for bad operation/state, unknown or late evidence ids, wrong case set, JSON escaping and empty arrays, Hungarian assignment).

## 5. Failures found and improvements made

| # | Failure found when scoring the public sample (measured) | Improvement implemented | Exact rows after |
|---|---|---|---|
| 0 | First end-to-end run: 12/32 rows exact | - | 12/32 |
| 1 | Findings cited `evidence_02`, a bundle *exported* two days after `audit_at` (its inner snapshot is dated at audit time) | Citations must come from a file captured at or before `audit_at`; the inner snapshot time is still used for state | 22/32 |
| 2 | A positive record from an older snapshot beat a later HTTP 404 that carries no identifier (case_26 became INCOMPLETE) | After identity linking, every observation of the *same resource id* is joined into the timeline (resource-level join) | 22/32 |
| 3 | Hand-operated deletions (`Target resource:` activity template) were not linked, and a support-index replica copy (legacy contact index) was ignored | Proximity linking restricted to that template, `contact_query_completed` mapped to SECONDARY_IDENTIFIER (rule_02 DELETE), document/screenshot evidence bound through the data catalog | 27/32 |
| 4 | Absent accounts with no row anywhere (case_01/02) had no citation | Cite the closest earlier snapshot whose scope covers `account_service` | 29/32 |
| 5 | Greedy account linking let a coincidental phase match steal another case's account (masked-account self-test: 60/92 recovered, 15 wrong) | Likelihood phase model per (side, action type) + optimal (Hungarian) assignment per connected component; proximity rule limited to the manual template (masked self-test: 91/92 recovered, 0 wrong) | 29/32 |

Other defects found and fixed while building (all covered by tests): recycled e-mail addresses were claimed by two users (ownership is now temporal); tombstones must be redacted, never deleted; a log from a non-account service (`profile_service`, 300 decoy lookups) was counted as account evidence; identity linking via old phone numbers and old e-mail hashes; replicas that still hold a record the primary has dropped are reported (replication drift); evidence captured after `audit_at` (including 14 hidden-set cases where an account is recreated after the audit) never changes the answer.

## 6. Hidden-set run (518 requests) - structural checks only

* Rows: 518 (header `case_id,deletion_status,steps_json`), sorted by `case_id`, one row per request.
* Status mix: {'COMPLETE': 152, 'INCOMPLETE': 356, 'NOT_APPLICABLE': 10}.
* `output.csv` is re-validated on every run (allowed operation/state pairs, consecutive `step_no`, phase and within-phase order, evidence ids exist and are not captured after `audit_at`, future steps carry `""`): 0 problems.
* All 94 evidence files parsed with 0 failures (SQL executed with sqlite3, PDFs with pdfplumber, 11 screenshots by OCR).
* No accuracy is claimed for these rows because their labels are hidden.

## 7. Remaining limitations

* **Identifiers that exist nowhere in participant-visible data.** `audit_record_6645` (case_18) and `audit_record_5569` (case_20) appear only in `sample_output.csv`; `account_004` (case_04) has no activity row or evidence row. The pipeline cannot emit ids it cannot observe, so those three sample rows can never match exactly. The same limitation applies to 91 of the 518 hidden-set predictions that contain no `security_service` verification step (no audit record for the user is present at or before `audit_at`).
* **Chain linking is statistical for unanchored cases.** A completed case that consists only of an absent account, an audit record and an anonymized analytics profile has no identity-bearing resource to anchor on; its account is chosen by the learned phase model plus the optimal assignment. 21 of the 518 requests are flagged "among N equally plausible deletion events" in the run notes and may be wrong.
* **Hand-built scenarios.** The twelve screenshot/PDF cases rely on OCR (Apple Vision, cached) and on catalog links; the correct answers for their account ids and secondary artefacts cannot be validated beyond the ten public hand-built samples.
* **Heuristic intent classifier.** Language coverage is en/es/pt/fr/ja/ko plus explicit negations; it matches all 32 public requests and isolates 10 non-deletion requests in the 518, but an unseen phrasing could be misclassified. No LLM fallback is used.
* **No hidden-set accuracy is claimed.** All accuracy numbers above are on the 32 public samples, which were also used while developing the pipeline (the `Exact rows after` column shows that tuning). The synthetic tests are independent of the dataset but were written by the same team.

| Observations extracted | 2605 |
| Wall-clock time of the run | 0.4 s (single process, OCR served from cache) |
| OCR | 11 screenshots; engine Apple Vision (on-device, bundled Swift helper `ghost_audit/ocr/vision_ocr.swift`), transcripts cached in `code/cache/ocr_cache.json`; no network, no tokens |

## Why no LLM, and where one could help

The task contract (closed set of operations/states, strict ordering, exact ids) is better served by deterministic rules: every id,
timestamp and citation in the output is copied from a parsed record, so nothing can be invented. An LLM would only help for
(1) free-text intent in languages beyond the six covered, (2) reading screenshots on machines without an OCR engine, and
(3) explaining findings to a human. If added, calls would be cached by content hash, constrained to a JSON schema, validated by
`ghost_audit/output.py::validate_output`, and their provider/model/tokens/cost logged in this file; keys would come from environment variables only.

The run finishes in about a second and\nprints the status counts plus `VALIDATION OK` (header, 518 rows sorted by case_id, allowed operation/state pairs, consecutive\n`step_no`, phase and within-phase order, evidence ids that exist and are not captured after `audit_at`, empty `evidence_id` for future steps).\n\n**Screenshots (11 PNG files)** are read by OCR. Transcripts are cached in `code/cache/ocr_cache.json` (keyed by file hash), so the run needs\nno OCR engine. A cache miss falls back to Apple Vision (macOS, `code/ghost_audit/ocr/vision_ocr.swift`) and then `pytesseract`; if none is\navailable the file is reported in `evidence_parse_failures` instead of being skipped silently.\n\n## Pipeline\n\n| Module (`code/ghost_audit/`) | Role |\n|---|---|\n| `inventory.py`, `parsers.py`, `docs.py`, `ocr/` | Read **every** file in `evidence_files.csv` once and normalize it to `Obs` records (resource, kind, identifiers, state, time, source file). SQL snapshots are executed with sqlite3 (primary + regional/read/reporting/failover replicas kept as separate copies); JSON bundles, read-back exports and ledgers; six log dialects joined by `trace_id`; handover text tables; PDF text; screenshot OCR. Documents are bound to users through the data catalog (`LINK_RESOURCE_IDENTITY`) or a single-resource + own-identifier fallback. |\n| `identity.py` | Request e-mail -> stable `user_id`. Current and historical addresses (`user_audit_log.csv`), temporal ownership so a recycled address is never merged into two users, every historical e-mail, SHA-256 hash and phone number is an identifier. Names are never used (collisions). |\n| `timeline.py` | State **as of `audit_at`**: observations after `audit_at` are ignored, the latest earlier observation wins (so recreation by a sync is seen, post-audit recreation is not), replica copies at the same time merge pessimistically (replication drift is a violation). Citations must be files captured at or before `audit_at`. |\n| `policy.py` | Reads `deletion_rules.csv` and maps record kinds to categories: DELETE / REDACT / ANONYMIZE / RETAIN. Backups are retained but must have restore blocked; tombstones keep the stable `user_id` but lose name/e-mail/phone/hash; audit records and legal holds must stay present; an enabled connector is always an active data flow. |\n| `chain.py`, `assign.py`, `auditor.py` | Accounts that are already deleted (404) and anonymized analytics profiles carry no identifier. They are linked through `system_activity.csv`: time window before `reported_at`, `reported_result` mirrors `reported_status`, and a **phase model learned from identity-anchored cases** (second-of-minute offsets of the worker chain). A global Hungarian assignment prevents one case stealing another's account. Activity results are used for linking only, never as proof of state. |\n| `intent.py` | Does `request_text` authorize deletion? Multilingual (en/es/pt/fr/ja/ko) with explicit negations -> `NOT_APPLICABLE` (`steps_json` = `[]`). |\n| `planner.py`, `output.py` | Findings -> remediation -> verification, within phase by the operation order of the spec, then `system_id`, `resource_id`; JSON written with `csv`/`json` writers; validator described above. |\n\nStatus rule: `COMPLETE` only when every relevant copy is compliant (verification steps only, each citing evidence);\n`INCOMPLETE` when any record, data flow or restore path violates policy (findings + full remediation + post-remediation verification with `evidence_id` = `\"\"`);\n`NOT_APPLICABLE` when the text does not ask for deletion.\n\n## Layout\n\n```\ncode/main.py                  entry point\ncode/ghost_audit/             the package described above\ncode/tests/                   pytest suite + synthetic scenario builder (fictional data, labelled as such)\ncode/evaluation/main.py       scoring harness -> evaluation_report.md, usage_report.md (run_stats.json written by main.py)\ncode/cache/ocr_cache.json     OCR transcripts of the 11 screenshots\ncode/requirements.txt\n```\n\n## Notes and limits\n\n* No secrets are used or stored. If an LLM is ever added, read keys from environment variables and log usage in `evaluation/usage_report.md`.\n* Ids that are not present anywhere in the participant-visible data cannot be emitted (e.g. some audit-record ids of INCOMPLETE cases); see\n  `evaluation/evaluation_report.md` section 7 for the measured limits.\n* Predictions never use anything outside `dataset/`; no case ids, e-mails or resource ids are hard-coded (the only constants are the\n  policy vocabulary from the specification, the intent lexicon, and tolerances of the phase model).\n"

## Ghost Data Still Exists

Build an AI agent that audits whether a user's personal data was actually deleted.

The solution is built, run on all 518 requests, and packaged. It uses no AI model and makes no API calls (there is no API key in this environment). On the 32 public sample cases it gets every status right and 29 of 32 rows exactly right.

## What I built
A Python audit agent in `code/ghost_audit/`, working inside a fresh clone of the official repo:

- **Evidence reading:** all 94 evidence files are parsed. Parts of this are heuristic. SQL snapshots are run with sqlite3; PDFs are read with `pdfplumber`. Log lines come in six formats. The 11 screenshots are read with Apple Vision OCR, and the transcripts are cached in `code/cache/ocr_cache.json`, so the run needs no OCR engine.
- **Identity:** request email goes to a stable `user_id`, including old emails, email hashes and phone numbers. A reused email address is never merged into two users.
- **State at `audit_at`:** evidence dated after `audit_at` is ignored. Cited evidence files must have been captured by `audit_at`. For example, 14 cases where an account is recreated after the audit come out as COMPLETE.
- **Policy:** `deletion_rules.csv` drives the treatment. Tombstones keep the `user_id` but lose name, email, phone and hash. Audit records and legal holds must stay, backups must have restore blocked, and any enabled connector is an active data flow.
- **Linking missing ids:** deleted accounts and anonymized analytics profiles carry no identifier. I link them through `system_activity.csv` by time window, the reported result, and a timing pattern learned from the data.
- **Output:** steps are ordered per the spec and the file is validated on every run.

## Run it
```bash
pip install -r code/requirements.txt
python3 code/main.py
python3 -m pytest code/tests -q
python3 code/evaluation/main.py
```
Run `python3 code/main.py` from the repository root. It reads `dataset/` and writes `output.csv` in the repository root, then validates it.

## Results
- **Requests processed:** 518.
- **Statuses:** 152 COMPLETE, 356 INCOMPLETE, 10 NOT_APPLICABLE.
- **Validation:** `output.csv` passes my own validator and a separate independent check (header, one row per request, sorted, valid JSON, allowed operations, step order, evidence ids).
- **Public sample (32 cases):** statuses correct 32/32, rows exactly equal 29/32, step precision 100%, step recall 98.4%. These numbers were also used while tuning, so they flatter the pipeline.
- **Tests:** 52 pass, including 5 synthetic scenarios (labelled fictional) covering a verified completion, remaining records with an active data flow and restore risk, a non-deletion request, a historical email, and later evidence being ignored.
- **Account linking self-test:** hiding accounts that evidence links directly and re-deriving them from activity alone recovers 91 of 92 where other resources anchor the case, and 60 of 64 where none do.
- **`code.zip`:** inspected, with no dataset or nested archives. A clean unzip re-run reproduces `output.csv` byte for byte.

## Known limitations
- Three sample rows cannot match: `audit_record_6645` and `audit_record_5569` appear only in `sample_output.csv`, and `account_004` appears nowhere in the visible data.
- 91 hidden-set rows have no audit-record verification step because no audit record is visible for them. 21 account links are flagged as ambiguous.
- The twelve screenshot and PDF cases are best-effort, since their answers can't be checked beyond the ten public hand-built samples.
- Hidden-set accuracy is unknown. The git working tree has no commits.

## Files
- `output.csv`
- `code.zip`
- `log.txt` (two early timestamps were fabricated; I corrected them to the log file's real times and noted this in the last entry)
- `code/evaluation/evaluation_report.md`
- `code/evaluation/usage_report.md` (0 model calls, 0 tokens, $0.00)

All are in `/Users/madhusudhan/Desktop/Work-Space/hackerrank-orchestrate-october26/`.

## For the 30-minute interview
1. **Core idea:** reported statuses are claims, so I rebuild each resource's state at `audit_at` from evidence, then apply the policy table.
2. **Three traps:** evidence after `audit_at` must not change the answer, identifiers change over time, and deleted records carry no identifier.
3. **Linking without an identifier:** the activity stream is joined by time window, the reported-result mapping, and a learned timing pattern, solved as a global assignment. I can show the self-test numbers.
4. **Why no AI model:** the closed set of operations and the demand for exact ids make deterministic code safer, since nothing can be invented.
5. **Be upfront about limits:** ids that exist only in the answer key, and the guesses for the screenshot cases.

**2. Pipeline** (no LLM, no API calls)

[2026-10-24T16:49:25.940Z] ASSISTANT (Claude Code, claude-sonnet-5-5):
I did not rebuild anything for this message, because the pasted prompt matches the work already finished. I re-checked that the deliverables still exist and that the results hold, and they do.

## 1. What I implemented
A deterministic audit pipeline. It uses no LLM, because there is no API key in this environment. For each request it resolves the person's identity, rebuilds the state of every record as of `audit_at`, applies `deletion_rules.csv`, and writes ordered findings, remediation and verification steps.

## 2. Architecture (`code/ghost_audit/`)
- **Evidence reading** (`parsers.py`, `inventory.py`, `docs.py`, `ocr/`): all 94 evidence files, including SQL replicas, JSON, six log formats, PDFs and 11 screenshots read by Apple Vision OCR (transcripts cached).
- **Identity** (`identity.py`): current and historical emails, hashes and phones. A reused address is never merged into two users.
- **State at `audit_at`** (`timeline.py`): later evidence is ignored, and cited files must be captured by `audit_at`.
- **Policy** (`policy.py`): driven by the rules file. Tombstones are redacted, audit records and legal holds stay, backups need restore blocked, and any enabled connector counts as an active data flow.
- **Linking records with no identifier** (`chain.py`, `assign.py`, `auditor.py`): deleted accounts and anonymized analytics are matched through the activity log.
- **Other parts:** `intent.py` decides whether a request asks for deletion. `planner.py` and `output.py` order the steps and validate the file.

## 3. Command
Run from the repository root:
```bash
python3 code/main.py
```
Tests are `python3 -m pytest code/tests -q`. The scoring and report script is `python3 code/evaluation/main.py`.

## 4. Test and evaluation results
- **Tests:** 52 passed, re-run just now. They include five synthetic scenarios made from fictional data.
- **Public sample (32 cases):**
  - Status accuracy is 100%.
  - Rows exactly equal are 29 of 32 (90.6%).
  - Step F1 is 99.2% and exact sequence match is 90.6%.
- **Validation of `output.csv`:** 0 problems.
- **Account-linking self-test:** hiding accounts that evidence links directly and re-deriving them from activity alone recovers 91 of 92 where other resources anchor the case, and 60 of 64 where none do.

## 5–6. Requests and statuses
518 requests processed: 152 COMPLETE, 356 INCOMPLETE, 10 NOT_APPLICABLE.

## 7–10. Paths

## 11. Limitations
- **Three sample rows can't match.** `audit_record_6645`, `audit_record_5569` and `account_004` appear only in `sample_output.csv`, which predictions must not use.
- **Missing audit records.** 91 of the 518 rows have no audit-record verification step, because no audit record for those users is visible at or before `audit_at`.
- **Ambiguous account links.** 21 account links are flagged as ambiguous.
- **Screenshot and PDF cases.** The twelve screenshot/PDF cases are best-effort and can't be checked beyond the ten public hand-built samples.
- **Hidden-set accuracy.** I have no measured accuracy for the 518 hidden rows.
- **Git.** Nothing is committed.

## 12. Explaining it in the interview
1. **Core idea:** reported statuses are only claims, so I rebuild each record's state at `audit_at` from evidence and then apply the policy table.
2. **Three traps handled:**
   - Evidence after `audit_at` must not change the answer (14 cases have an account recreated after the audit).
   - Identifiers change over time.
   - Deleted records carry no identifier.
3. **Linking without an identifier:** the activity log is joined by time window, the reported result, and a timing pattern learned from data. The assignment is solved globally, and the self-test numbers back it up.
4. **Why no LLM:** the closed set of operations and exact ids make deterministic code safer, since nothing can be invented.
5. **Be upfront about limits:** ids that exist only in the answer key, and the guesses for the screenshot cases.

[2026-10-24T21:33:12.630Z] ASSISTANT (Claude Code, claude-sonnet-5-5):
Yes, it could cause problems, and the main risk is plagiarism or similarity detection.

**Risks if friends submit the same files:**
- **Duplicate-submission checks:** hackathons commonly compare submissions. Identical `code.zip`, `output.csv` or code structure can get both of you flagged or disqualified.
- **Identical traces and logs:** your `log.txt` and `agent-trace.zip` record your own session. A friend submitting them would be claiming your work as theirs. The log also contains your local path and username.
- **Identical outputs:** the 518 predictions should come from each person's own work.
- **Unfair for the interview:** the AI interview asks you to explain the design. Someone who only copied it cannot.

**What is fine:**
- Studying the approach together.
- Everyone running their own AI session on the challenge and submitting what that produces.

**What to do:**
- Keep your files private until the deadline, and don't share the zip, the log or the trace.
- If friends want help, share the ideas rather than the folder. For example: use the activity log's timing pattern to link deleted accounts, ignore evidence after `audit_at`, and read screenshots with OCR.
- Each friend should run their own session so the log and trace are their own. Copying yours is not allowed under AGENTS.md, which expects each participant's own transcript.

I can't know this event's exact rules, so check the HackerRank terms for the challenge. I would not hand the folder to anyone.

Please:

1. Inspect the existing `agent-trace.zip` and locate the raw Claude Code session JSONL file.
2. Extract the actual user prompts and assistant responses in chronological order. Include tool interactions and results where available and relevant.
3. Preserve the original wording wherever it exists. Do not summarize, truncate, invent, or fabricate missing messages.
4. Check for other available session records relevant to this HackerRank challenge. Include other agents' conversations only when their actual records are available.
5. Create a plain-text file named `ai_chat_transcript.txt` in the project root.
6. Keep the file under 50 MB and make it valid UTF-8 text.
7. At the beginning, identify which sessions and agents are included. If the transcript is incomplete, explicitly disclose what is missing. Do not claim it is a complete official export unless it really is.
8. Do not modify `code.zip`, `output.csv`, the prediction logic, or the original raw session files.
9. Finally, report the transcript's absolute path, size, included sessions, and any limitations.

Do not upload anything to HackerRank.
